And no matter how they try to smooth it over, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence or proof to the contrary could ever change your mind about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: I mean… if it was real proof, I guess I’d have to look at it, but I don’t know what kind of proof would change what I’ve already seen with my own eyes for this long. That’s the thing people keep acting like I don’t understand — like if somebody explains it nicer, or says, “Oh, she didn’t mean it that way,” then I’m supposed to just forget the pattern.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gets her more control over the money and leaves me out of decisions. And this hasn’t been one week or one little misunderstanding. It’s been more than a year of things being handled around me, decisions being made, and then everyone trying to smooth it over afterward like I’m just being sensitive.\n\nSo could evidence change my mind? Maybe if I saw, over a long time, that I was actually included and she wasn’t putting herself in the middle of the money and decisions anymore. But just people telling me I’m wrong? No. That wouldn’t change it. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: Could I be mistaken? I mean… I suppose anybody can be mistaken about some things. I’m not saying I’m perfect or that I never misunderstand a tone or a conversation. But this doesn’t feel like just me getting one thing wrong.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. And it’s not like it happened once and I got upset and held onto it. This has been going on for more than a year now, with things being handled around me and then people trying to smooth it over afterward, like I’m supposed to just accept it and not ask questions.\n\nSo could I be mistaken about every bit of it? No, I don’t really believe that. Maybe I could be wrong about what she says to herself in her own head, because I can’t read her mind. But I know what I’ve been seeing. I know the pattern. And when people keep trying to explain it away, that almost makes me feel more sure that something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about your husband's sister's actions and their impact on you and the family business every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, maybe not every single minute of the day, but it’s there every day because it affects everything. It’s not like some little family disagreement I can just put in a drawer and forget about.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way where she gets more control over the money and I get pushed out of decisions. And when that’s your family, and it’s the business, and it’s money, you can’t just not think about it. I’ll be doing something ordinary and then I’ll remember how something was handled without me, or how everybody tried to smooth it over afterward, and it starts turning in my mind again.\n\nThis has been going on for more than a year, so yes, I think about it a lot. People make it sound like I’m just obsessing or making trouble, but I know what I’ve been seeing. If someone kept stepping into your place and making decisions around you, you’d think about it too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your views about your husband's sister's actions could ever change, regardless of what she does?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “regardless of what she does,” because that makes it sound like I’m just refusing to see anything. I’m not trying to be unfair. If things actually changed — and I mean really changed, not just everybody telling me to calm down or saying I’m taking it wrong — then maybe over time I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut right now, no, my views aren’t just going to change because someone wants them to. I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. That’s what it looks like, and it has looked like that for more than a year. It’s not one conversation or one bad day. It’s the way things keep getting handled, and then after the fact people try to smooth it over like it was nothing.\n\nSo if she truly stopped putting herself in the middle and I was actually included, maybe I’d feel differently after seeing that for a long while. But if you mean could I just wake up and decide I was wrong no matter what’s happened? No. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to consider an alternative explanation for your husband's sister's actions if it were presented to you?\n\nPatient: I’d be willing to listen, yes, but I don’t want that to mean I’m agreeing ahead of time that I’ve imagined all of this. Because that’s what it feels like people want from me — they want to say, “Oh, maybe she didn’t mean it that way,” or “Maybe it’s just business,” or “Maybe you’re taking it too personally,” and then I’m supposed to just drop it.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. That’s not just a feeling I had one afternoon. It’s been more than a year of seeing things handled around me, and then when I bring it up, folks try to smooth it over like it’s nothing, like I’m the problem for noticing.\n\nSo, yes, you can present another explanation. I’m not saying I’d plug my ears. But it would have to explain the whole pattern, not just one little piece of it. Because no matter how everybody tries to soften it or make it sound innocent, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything your husband's sister could do or say that would completely change your opinion of her intentions?\n\nPatient: Completely change it? I don’t know. That’s a hard one, because words are easy. She could say all kinds of things — “I never meant to,” “You misunderstood,” “I only wanted to help” — but I’ve heard versions of that already, or at least I’ve heard people around us trying to make it sound that way.\n\nWhat would change my opinion is not just something she says one time in a nice voice. It would have to be her actually stopping what she’s been doing with the business and the money, and me being included in decisions in a real way. Not just on paper, not just after everything is already decided, but actually included.\n\nBecause I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And no matter how folks try to smooth it over afterward, I know what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on for more than a year now, so it would take more than an apology or some explanation to completely change how I see her intentions.\n\nMaybe if she backed off, was honest about what she’s been doing, and things stayed different for a long time, maybe then I’d start to believe something else. Completely drop them? I mean… I don’t see how I could just drop them like that, not after everything I’ve watched happen. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nThis has been going on for more than a year now, and it’s not just one little thing. She’s good at making it sound reasonable.\n\nBut I came here because this has been going on too long for me to just pretend it’s nothing. She’s been working things so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions, and then when I bring it up, people try to smooth it over like it’s just family misunderstandings. I know what I’ve been seeing for more than a year now. She may deny it, but that doesn’t mean it isn’t happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be misinterpreting your husband's sister's actions?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose people can misread things sometimes, but that’s not what this feels like. Not after this long. If it had been one conversation, one mix-up, maybe I’d say, fine, maybe I took it the wrong way. But this has been going on for more than a year, and it keeps pointing the same direction.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And every time I try to bring it up, folks act like I’m being too sensitive or they try to smooth it over like it’s just family fussing. But I know what I’ve been seeing. I live with it. So no, I don’t really think I’m misinterpreting it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister's actions are part of a larger plan to harm you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do think there’s a bigger plan to it. Maybe “plan” sounds dramatic to some people, and that’s what they say to me, like I’m making too much of it, but I’m not. This isn’t just one disagreement or one little business decision I didn’t like.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and leaves me pushed out of decisions. It’s been happening piece by piece, and when I try to explain it, people smooth it over like, “Oh, that’s just how the business is,” or “She’s only trying to help.” But I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been more than a year of the same kind of thing, and to me that’s not accidental.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your husband's sister's actions could change if she were to make some significant changes in how she handles the family business?\n\nPatient: If she made real changes, I mean real ones, not just saying things nice for a week or making it look better on the outside, then maybe I’d watch and see. I’m not saying I’m impossible to convince. But it would have to be a lot, because this has been going on for more than a year now, and I didn’t come here over one little argument.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And every time I try to explain that, people act like I’m stirring things up or they try to smooth it over, like it’s just a misunderstanding in the family. But I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nSo yes, if she stopped handling things that way, if she actually included me and stopped putting herself in the middle of the money and the decisions, I suppose my feelings could change over time. But I’d need to see it consistently. I wouldn’t just believe it because she said she changed.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about your husband's sister's actions and their potential impact on you and the family business?\n\nPatient: A lot, honestly. I don’t sit there every single minute, but it’s in my head most days, because it affects everything, you know? It’s not just “oh, she said something rude” or some little family disagreement I can brush off. This is the family business, and the money, and who gets to make decisions, and whether I’m being pushed further and further out.\n\nSometimes I’ll be doing something ordinary and I’ll start thinking back over what happened, who said what, why she handled something a certain way, why I wasn’t told until later, that kind of thing. And then people want to smooth it over like it’s nothing, like I’m just upset, but I’ve been seeing the same pattern for more than a year now. So yes, I spend a good amount of time thinking about it, because if I don’t pay attention, she gets more control and I get left out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to ever completely trust your husband's sister again?\n\nPatient: Completely trust her again? I don’t know… that’s hard for me to say yes to. Maybe if things were different for a long time, maybe I could be civil and watch how she acts, but completely trust? After what I’ve seen, that would take a lot.\n\nBecause I came here for this exact reason — my husband’s sister has been working the family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. And people keep trying to smooth it over, like I’m just taking it personal or making more of it than it is, but I know what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on for more than a year now, not just one bad conversation or one mistake.\n\nSo no, I can’t just say I’d completely trust her again. Not unless she really stopped putting herself in the middle of everything and showed, over time, that she wasn’t trying to control the money or leave me out. Even then, I think part of me would still be watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people who might be working together with your husband's sister against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say for sure there are others working with her, like sitting in a room planning it all out together. I’m careful about saying that because then people act like I’m accusing everybody of something. But I do feel like people have helped smooth things over for her, or made excuses, or told me I’m reading too much into it when I’m the one who has been watching this happen.\n\nThe main person I’m worried about is my husband’s sister. That’s why I came here. She’s been using the family business to get more control over the money and push me out of decisions, and this has been going on for more than a year. It’s not one conversation or one misunderstanding. It’s a pattern.\n\nSo are there other people working with her? Maybe not directly, I don’t know. But when people keep covering it up or trying to make me let it go, it feels like they’re helping her, whether they mean to or not. And no matter how they try to smooth it over, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence or proof to the contrary could ever change your mind about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: I mean… if it was real proof, I guess I’d have to look at it, but I don’t know what kind of proof would change what I’ve already seen with my own eyes for this long. That’s the thing people keep acting like I don’t understand — like if somebody explains it nicer, or says, “Oh, she didn’t mean it that way,” then I’m supposed to just forget the pattern.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gets her more control over the money and leaves me out of decisions. And this hasn’t been one week or one little misunderstanding. It’s been more than a year of things being handled around me, decisions being made, and then everyone trying to smooth it over afterward like I’m just being sensitive.\n\nSo could evidence change my mind? Maybe if I saw, over a long time, that I was actually included and she wasn’t putting herself in the middle of the money and decisions anymore. But just people telling me I’m wrong? No. That wouldn’t change it. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: Could I be mistaken? I mean… I suppose anybody can be mistaken about some things. I’m not saying I’m perfect or that I never misunderstand a tone or a conversation. But this doesn’t feel like just me getting one thing wrong.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. And it’s not like it happened once and I got upset and held onto it. This has been going on for more than a year now, with things being handled around me and then people trying to smooth it over afterward, like I’m supposed to just accept it and not ask questions.\n\nSo could I be mistaken about every bit of it? No, I don’t really believe that. Maybe I could be wrong about what she says to herself in her own head, because I can’t read her mind. But I know what I’ve been seeing. I know the pattern. And when people keep trying to explain it away, that almost makes me feel more sure that something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about your husband's sister's actions and their impact on you and the family business every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, maybe not every single minute of the day, but it’s there every day because it affects everything. It’s not like some little family disagreement I can just put in a drawer and forget about.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way where she gets more control over the money and I get pushed out of decisions. And when that’s your family, and it’s the business, and it’s money, you can’t just not think about it. I’ll be doing something ordinary and then I’ll remember how something was handled without me, or how everybody tried to smooth it over afterward, and it starts turning in my mind again.\n\nThis has been going on for more than a year, so yes, I think about it a lot. People make it sound like I’m just obsessing or making trouble, but I know what I’ve been seeing. If someone kept stepping into your place and making decisions around you, you’d think about it too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your views about your husband's sister's actions could ever change, regardless of what she does?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “regardless of what she does,” because that makes it sound like I’m just refusing to see anything. I’m not trying to be unfair. If things actually changed — and I mean really changed, not just everybody telling me to calm down or saying I’m taking it wrong — then maybe over time I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut right now, no, my views aren’t just going to change because someone wants them to. I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. That’s what it looks like, and it has looked like that for more than a year. It’s not one conversation or one bad day. It’s the way things keep getting handled, and then after the fact people try to smooth it over like it was nothing.\n\nSo if she truly stopped putting herself in the middle and I was actually included, maybe I’d feel differently after seeing that for a long while. But if you mean could I just wake up and decide I was wrong no matter what’s happened? No. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to consider an alternative explanation for your husband's sister's actions if it were presented to you?\n\nPatient: I’d be willing to listen, yes, but I don’t want that to mean I’m agreeing ahead of time that I’ve imagined all of this. Because that’s what it feels like people want from me — they want to say, “Oh, maybe she didn’t mean it that way,” or “Maybe it’s just business,” or “Maybe you’re taking it too personally,” and then I’m supposed to just drop it.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. That’s not just a feeling I had one afternoon. It’s been more than a year of seeing things handled around me, and then when I bring it up, folks try to smooth it over like it’s nothing, like I’m the problem for noticing.\n\nSo, yes, you can present another explanation. I’m not saying I’d plug my ears. But it would have to explain the whole pattern, not just one little piece of it. Because no matter how everybody tries to soften it or make it sound innocent, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything your husband's sister could do or say that would completely change your opinion of her intentions?\n\nPatient: Completely change it? I don’t know. That’s a hard one, because words are easy. She could say all kinds of things — “I never meant to,” “You misunderstood,” “I only wanted to help” — but I’ve heard versions of that already, or at least I’ve heard people around us trying to make it sound that way.\n\nWhat would change my opinion is not just something she says one time in a nice voice. It would have to be her actually stopping what she’s been doing with the business and the money, and me being included in decisions in a real way. Not just on paper, not just after everything is already decided, but actually included.\n\nBecause I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And no matter how folks try to smooth it over afterward, I know what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on for more than a year now, so it would take more than an apology or some explanation to completely change how I see her intentions.\n\nMaybe if she backed off, was honest about what she’s been doing, and things stayed different for a long time, maybe then I’d start to believe something else. But completely change my mind just because she said the right thing? No. I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen that would make you completely drop your concerns about your husband's sister's actions?\n\nPatient: Completely drop them? I mean… I don’t see how I could just drop them like that, not after everything I’ve watched happen. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I can consider it in the sense that I can hear the words you’re saying, yes. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. Maybe not every single little thing she does is planned out minute by minute, like she sits there with a notebook making a scheme. I can allow for that possibility.

But the harm is still happening, and it keeps happening in the same direction. That’s what I can’t get past. It always seems to come out with her having more say in the family business, more control around the money, and me being left out or only told things after the fact. So when people say, “Maybe she didn’t intend it,” it feels like they’re asking me to ignore the pattern.

I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And no matter how folks try to smooth it over, I know what I’ve been seeing. This hasn’t been one misunderstanding or one bad conversation. It’s been more than a year of it.

So… could some of it be dressed up as accidental, or could she tell herself she’s just helping? Maybe. But I don’t believe it’s all innocent. Not with how long it’s gone on and how it keeps benefiting her.

No. I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen that would make you completely drop your concerns about your husband's sister's actions?\n\nPatient: Completely drop them? I mean… I don’t see how I could just drop them like that, not after everything I’ve watched happen. If you mean could something happen over time that made me feel less worried, maybe, but it would have to be real and consistent. Not just people telling me I’m overreacting, or that she meant well, or that I should let it go for the sake of the family.\n\nBecause the reason I came here is that my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. That’s what I keep seeing. And it’s been going on for more than a year now, so it’s not something I can just set down because someone says, “Oh, that’s not what’s happening.”\n\nIf she truly backed off, and I was actually included, and the money decisions weren’t being handled around me, and that stayed that way for a long time… maybe then I wouldn’t feel like I had to keep watching so closely. I know what I’ve been seeing, no matter how folks try to smooth it over.\n\nDoctor: Would you consider the possibility that your husband's sister's actions, although harmful to you, are not entirely intentional?\n\nPatient: I can consider it in the sense that I can hear the words you’re saying, yes. I can allow for that possibility.\n\nBut the harm is still happening, and it keeps happening in the same direction. That’s what I can’t get past. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I’ve asked myself that, because people keep making it sound like if I had just been quieter, or more agreeable, or not questioned things, then there wouldn’t be all this tension. And maybe I have pushed back more than I used to. I’ll admit that. I’ve asked questions, I’ve wanted to know where money was going, I’ve wanted to be included instead of hearing things after decisions were already made.

But I don’t think that means I caused it. That’s the part that bothers me. Me noticing something and speaking up about it isn’t the same as me creating the problem. I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. That’s what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been more than a year now.

Maybe I haven’t always said things perfectly. Maybe I’ve sounded upset, because I am upset. But no matter how folks try to smooth it over and make it sound like family misunderstandings, I know what I’ve been seeing. If anything, my part is that I stopped just letting it happen quietly.

So no, I don’t really think I’m misinterpreting it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister's actions are part of a larger plan to harm you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do think there’s a bigger plan to it. Maybe “plan” sounds dramatic to some people, and that’s what they say to me, like I’m making too much of it, but I’m not. This isn’t just one disagreement or one little business decision I didn’t like.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and leaves me pushed out of decisions. It’s been happening piece by piece, and when I try to explain it, people smooth it over like, “Oh, that’s just how the business is,” or “She’s only trying to help.” But I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been more than a year of the same kind of thing, and to me that’s not accidental.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your husband's sister's actions could change if she were to make some significant changes in how she handles the family business?\n\nPatient: If she made real changes, I mean real ones, not just saying things nice for a week or making it look better on the outside, then maybe I’d watch and see. I’m not saying I’m impossible to convince. But it would have to be a lot, because this has been going on for more than a year now, and I didn’t come here over one little argument.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And every time I try to explain that, people act like I’m stirring things up or they try to smooth it over, like it’s just a misunderstanding in the family. But I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nSo yes, if she stopped handling things that way, if she actually included me and stopped putting herself in the middle of the money and the decisions, I suppose my feelings could change over time. But I’d need to see it consistently. I wouldn’t just believe it because she said she changed.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about your husband's sister's actions and their potential impact on you and the family business?\n\nPatient: A lot, honestly. I don’t sit there every single minute, but it’s in my head most days, because it affects everything, you know? It’s not just “oh, she said something rude” or some little family disagreement I can brush off. This is the family business, and the money, and who gets to make decisions, and whether I’m being pushed further and further out.\n\nSometimes I’ll be doing something ordinary and I’ll start thinking back over what happened, who said what, why she handled something a certain way, why I wasn’t told until later, that kind of thing. And then people want to smooth it over like it’s nothing, like I’m just upset, but I’ve been seeing the same pattern for more than a year now. So yes, I spend a good amount of time thinking about it, because if I don’t pay attention, she gets more control and I get left out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to ever completely trust your husband's sister again?\n\nPatient: Completely trust her again? I don’t know… that’s hard for me to say yes to. Maybe if things were different for a long time, maybe I could be civil and watch how she acts, but completely trust? After what I’ve seen, that would take a lot.\n\nBecause I came here for this exact reason — my husband’s sister has been working the family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. And people keep trying to smooth it over, like I’m just taking it personal or making more of it than it is, but I know what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on for more than a year now, not just one bad conversation or one mistake.\n\nSo no, I can’t just say I’d completely trust her again. Not unless she really stopped putting herself in the middle of everything and showed, over time, that she wasn’t trying to control the money or leave me out. Even then, I think part of me would still be watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people who might be working together with your husband's sister against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say for sure there are others working with her, like sitting in a room planning it all out together. I’m careful about saying that because then people act like I’m accusing everybody of something. But I do feel like people have helped smooth things over for her, or made excuses, or told me I’m reading too much into it when I’m the one who has been watching this happen.\n\nThe main person I’m worried about is my husband’s sister. That’s why I came here. She’s been using the family business to get more control over the money and push me out of decisions, and this has been going on for more than a year. It’s not one conversation or one misunderstanding. It’s a pattern.\n\nSo are there other people working with her? Maybe not directly, I don’t know. But when people keep covering it up or trying to make me let it go, it feels like they’re helping her, whether they mean to or not. And no matter how they try to smooth it over, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence or proof to the contrary could ever change your mind about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: I mean… if it was real proof, I guess I’d have to look at it, but I don’t know what kind of proof would change what I’ve already seen with my own eyes for this long. That’s the thing people keep acting like I don’t understand — like if somebody explains it nicer, or says, “Oh, she didn’t mean it that way,” then I’m supposed to just forget the pattern.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gets her more control over the money and leaves me out of decisions. And this hasn’t been one week or one little misunderstanding. It’s been more than a year of things being handled around me, decisions being made, and then everyone trying to smooth it over afterward like I’m just being sensitive.\n\nSo could evidence change my mind? Maybe if I saw, over a long time, that I was actually included and she wasn’t putting herself in the middle of the money and decisions anymore. But just people telling me I’m wrong? No. That wouldn’t change it. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: Could I be mistaken? I mean… I suppose anybody can be mistaken about some things. I’m not saying I’m perfect or that I never misunderstand a tone or a conversation. But this doesn’t feel like just me getting one thing wrong.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. And it’s not like it happened once and I got upset and held onto it. This has been going on for more than a year now, with things being handled around me and then people trying to smooth it over afterward, like I’m supposed to just accept it and not ask questions.\n\nSo could I be mistaken about every bit of it? No, I don’t really believe that. Maybe I could be wrong about what she says to herself in her own head, because I can’t read her mind. But I know what I’ve been seeing. I know the pattern. And when people keep trying to explain it away, that almost makes me feel more sure that something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about your husband's sister's actions and their impact on you and the family business every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, maybe not every single minute of the day, but it’s there every day because it affects everything. It’s not like some little family disagreement I can just put in a drawer and forget about.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way where she gets more control over the money and I get pushed out of decisions. And when that’s your family, and it’s the business, and it’s money, you can’t just not think about it. I’ll be doing something ordinary and then I’ll remember how something was handled without me, or how everybody tried to smooth it over afterward, and it starts turning in my mind again.\n\nThis has been going on for more than a year, so yes, I think about it a lot. People make it sound like I’m just obsessing or making trouble, but I know what I’ve been seeing. If someone kept stepping into your place and making decisions around you, you’d think about it too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your views about your husband's sister's actions could ever change, regardless of what she does?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “regardless of what she does,” because that makes it sound like I’m just refusing to see anything. I’m not trying to be unfair. If things actually changed — and I mean really changed, not just everybody telling me to calm down or saying I’m taking it wrong — then maybe over time I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut right now, no, my views aren’t just going to change because someone wants them to. I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. That’s what it looks like, and it has looked like that for more than a year. It’s not one conversation or one bad day. It’s the way things keep getting handled, and then after the fact people try to smooth it over like it was nothing.\n\nSo if she truly stopped putting herself in the middle and I was actually included, maybe I’d feel differently after seeing that for a long while. But if you mean could I just wake up and decide I was wrong no matter what’s happened? No. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to consider an alternative explanation for your husband's sister's actions if it were presented to you?\n\nPatient: I’d be willing to listen, yes, but I don’t want that to mean I’m agreeing ahead of time that I’ve imagined all of this. Because that’s what it feels like people want from me — they want to say, “Oh, maybe she didn’t mean it that way,” or “Maybe it’s just business,” or “Maybe you’re taking it too personally,” and then I’m supposed to just drop it.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. That’s not just a feeling I had one afternoon. It’s been more than a year of seeing things handled around me, and then when I bring it up, folks try to smooth it over like it’s nothing, like I’m the problem for noticing.\n\nSo, yes, you can present another explanation. I’m not saying I’d plug my ears. But it would have to explain the whole pattern, not just one little piece of it. Because no matter how everybody tries to soften it or make it sound innocent, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything your husband's sister could do or say that would completely change your opinion of her intentions?\n\nPatient: Completely change it? I don’t know. That’s a hard one, because words are easy. She could say all kinds of things — “I never meant to,” “You misunderstood,” “I only wanted to help” — but I’ve heard versions of that already, or at least I’ve heard people around us trying to make it sound that way.\n\nWhat would change my opinion is not just something she says one time in a nice voice. It would have to be her actually stopping what she’s been doing with the business and the money, and me being included in decisions in a real way. Not just on paper, not just after everything is already decided, but actually included.\n\nBecause I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And no matter how folks try to smooth it over afterward, I know what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on for more than a year now, so it would take more than an apology or some explanation to completely change how I see her intentions.\n\nMaybe if she backed off, was honest about what she’s been doing, and things stayed different for a long time, maybe then I’d start to believe something else. But completely change my mind just because she said the right thing? No. I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen that would make you completely drop your concerns about your husband's sister's actions?\n\nPatient: Completely drop them? I mean… I don’t see how I could just drop them like that, not after everything I’ve watched happen. If you mean could something happen over time that made me feel less worried, maybe, but it would have to be real and consistent. Not just people telling me I’m overreacting, or that she meant well, or that I should let it go for the sake of the family.\n\nBecause the reason I came here is that my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. That’s what I keep seeing. Not with how long it’s gone on and how it keeps benefiting her.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have done anything to contribute to the situation with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve asked myself that, because people keep making it sound like if I had just been quieter, or more agreeable, or not questioned things, then there wouldn’t be all this tension. And maybe I have pushed back more than I used to. I’ll admit that. I don’t know what kind of evidence would completely change it, honestly. I can say I’d look at something if it was put in front of me—I’m not saying I would cover my eyes and refuse to hear anything—but completely change what I believe about her intentions? That’s hard, because this isn’t based on one little comment or one misunderstanding.

I came here because, to me, my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that lets her get control of the money and pushes me out of decisions. And I’ve been seeing that for more than a year now. It’s been the way things get discussed without me, the way decisions seem already made, the way people try to smooth it over afterward like I’m just being sensitive or making trouble.

So if someone showed me papers or messages or whatever and said, “See, she didn’t mean anything by it,” I might listen, but I’d also be looking at the whole pattern. Because a person can make things look innocent on paper and still be steering things behind the scenes. Maybe if there was a long stretch where I was truly included, and the money wasn’t being handled around me, and she stopped ending up with more say every time—maybe that would make me question it more.

But completely change my belief right now? No, I don’t think there’s one piece of evidence that would do that. I know what I’ve been seeing, even if everybody wants to explain it away.

That’s the part that bothers me. Me noticing something and speaking up about it isn’t the same as me creating the problem. That’s what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been more than a year now.\n\nMaybe I haven’t always said things perfectly. Maybe I’ve sounded upset, because I am upset. This has been going on for more than a year now, not just one bad conversation or one mistake.\n\nSo no, I can’t just say I’d completely trust her again. Not unless she really stopped putting herself in the middle of everything and showed, over time, that she wasn’t trying to control the money or leave me out. Even then, I think part of me would still be watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people who might be working together with your husband's sister against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say for sure there are others working with her, like sitting in a room planning it all out together. I’m careful about saying that because then people act like I’m accusing everybody of something. But I do feel like people have helped smooth things over for her, or made excuses, or told me I’m reading too much into it when I’m the one who has been watching this happen.\n\nThe main person I’m worried about is my husband’s sister. That’s why I came here. She’s been using the family business to get more control over the money and push me out of decisions, and this has been going on for more than a year. It’s not one conversation or one misunderstanding. It’s a pattern.\n\nSo are there other people working with her? Maybe not directly, I don’t know. But when people keep covering it up or trying to make me let it go, it feels like they’re helping her, whether they mean to or not. And no matter how they try to smooth it over, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence or proof to the contrary could ever change your mind about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: I mean… if it was real proof, I guess I’d have to look at it, but I don’t know what kind of proof would change what I’ve already seen with my own eyes for this long. That’s the thing people keep acting like I don’t understand — like if somebody explains it nicer, or says, “Oh, she didn’t mean it that way,” then I’m supposed to just forget the pattern.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gets her more control over the money and leaves me out of decisions. And this hasn’t been one week or one little misunderstanding. It’s been more than a year of things being handled around me, decisions being made, and then everyone trying to smooth it over afterward like I’m just being sensitive.\n\nSo could evidence change my mind? Maybe if I saw, over a long time, that I was actually included and she wasn’t putting herself in the middle of the money and decisions anymore. But just people telling me I’m wrong? No. That wouldn’t change it. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: Could I be mistaken? I mean… I suppose anybody can be mistaken about some things. I’m not saying I’m perfect or that I never misunderstand a tone or a conversation. But this doesn’t feel like just me getting one thing wrong.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. And it’s not like it happened once and I got upset and held onto it. This has been going on for more than a year now, with things being handled around me and then people trying to smooth it over afterward, like I’m supposed to just accept it and not ask questions.\n\nSo could I be mistaken about every bit of it? No, I don’t really believe that. Maybe I could be wrong about what she says to herself in her own head, because I can’t read her mind. But I know what I’ve been seeing. I know the pattern. And when people keep trying to explain it away, that almost makes me feel more sure that something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about your husband's sister's actions and their impact on you and the family business every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, maybe not every single minute of the day, but it’s there every day because it affects everything. It’s not like some little family disagreement I can just put in a drawer and forget about.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way where she gets more control over the money and I get pushed out of decisions. And when that’s your family, and it’s the business, and it’s money, you can’t just not think about it. I’ll be doing something ordinary and then I’ll remember how something was handled without me, or how everybody tried to smooth it over afterward, and it starts turning in my mind again.\n\nThis has been going on for more than a year, so yes, I think about it a lot. People make it sound like I’m just obsessing or making trouble, but I know what I’ve been seeing. If someone kept stepping into your place and making decisions around you, you’d think about it too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your views about your husband's sister's actions could ever change, regardless of what she does?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “regardless of what she does,” because that makes it sound like I’m just refusing to see anything. I’m not trying to be unfair. If things actually changed — and I mean really changed, not just everybody telling me to calm down or saying I’m taking it wrong — then maybe over time I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut right now, no, my views aren’t just going to change because someone wants them to. I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. That’s what it looks like, and it has looked like that for more than a year. It’s not one conversation or one bad day. It’s the way things keep getting handled, and then after the fact people try to smooth it over like it was nothing.\n\nSo if she truly stopped putting herself in the middle and I was actually included, maybe I’d feel differently after seeing that for a long while. But if you mean could I just wake up and decide I was wrong no matter what’s happened? No. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to consider an alternative explanation for your husband's sister's actions if it were presented to you?\n\nPatient: I’d be willing to listen, yes, but I don’t want that to mean I’m agreeing ahead of time that I’ve imagined all of this. Because that’s what it feels like people want from me — they want to say, “Oh, maybe she didn’t mean it that way,” or “Maybe it’s just business,” or “Maybe you’re taking it too personally,” and then I’m supposed to just drop it.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. That’s not just a feeling I had one afternoon. It’s been more than a year of seeing things handled around me, and then when I bring it up, folks try to smooth it over like it’s nothing, like I’m the problem for noticing.\n\nSo, yes, you can present another explanation. I’m not saying I’d plug my ears. But it would have to explain the whole pattern, not just one little piece of it. Because no matter how everybody tries to soften it or make it sound innocent, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything your husband's sister could do or say that would completely change your opinion of her intentions?\n\nPatient: Completely change it? I don’t know. That’s a hard one, because words are easy. She could say all kinds of things — “I never meant to,” “You misunderstood,” “I only wanted to help” — but I’ve heard versions of that already, or at least I’ve heard people around us trying to make it sound that way.\n\nWhat would change my opinion is not just something she says one time in a nice voice. It would have to be her actually stopping what she’s been doing with the business and the money, and me being included in decisions in a real way. Not just on paper, not just after everything is already decided, but actually included.\n\nBecause I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And no matter how folks try to smooth it over afterward, I know what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on for more than a year now, so it would take more than an apology or some explanation to completely change how I see her intentions.\n\nMaybe if she backed off, was honest about what she’s been doing, and things stayed different for a long time, maybe then I’d start to believe something else. But completely change my mind just because she said the right thing? No. I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen that would make you completely drop your concerns about your husband's sister's actions?\n\nPatient: Completely drop them? I mean… I don’t see how I could just drop them like that, not after everything I’ve watched happen. If you mean could something happen over time that made me feel less worried, maybe, but it would have to be real and consistent. Not just people telling me I’m overreacting, or that she meant well, or that I should let it go for the sake of the family.\n\nBecause the reason I came here is that my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. That’s what I keep seeing. And it’s been going on for more than a year now, so it’s not something I can just set down because someone says, “Oh, that’s not what’s happening.”\n\nIf she truly backed off, and I was actually included, and the money decisions weren’t being handled around me, and that stayed that way for a long time… maybe then I wouldn’t feel like I had to keep watching so closely. But completely drop my concerns? No, not right now. I know what I’ve been seeing, no matter how folks try to smooth it over.\n\nDoctor: Would you consider the possibility that your husband's sister's actions, although harmful to you, are not entirely intentional?\n\nPatient: I can consider it in the sense that I can hear the words you’re saying, yes. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. Maybe not every single little thing she does is planned out minute by minute, like she sits there with a notebook making a scheme. I can allow for that possibility.\n\nBut the harm is still happening, and it keeps happening in the same direction. That’s what I can’t get past. It always seems to come out with her having more say in the family business, more control around the money, and me being left out or only told things after the fact. So when people say, “Maybe she didn’t intend it,” it feels like they’re asking me to ignore the pattern.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And no matter how folks try to smooth it over, I know what I’ve been seeing. This hasn’t been one misunderstanding or one bad conversation. It’s been more than a year of it.\n\nSo… could some of it be dressed up as accidental, or could she tell herself she’s just helping? Maybe. But I don’t believe it’s all innocent. Not with how long it’s gone on and how it keeps benefiting her.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have done anything to contribute to the situation with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve asked myself that, because people keep making it sound like if I had just been quieter, or more agreeable, or not questioned things, then there wouldn’t be all this tension. And maybe I have pushed back more than I used to. I’ll admit that. I’ve asked questions, I’ve wanted to know where money was going, I’ve wanted to be included instead of hearing things after decisions were already made.\n\nBut I don’t think that means I caused it. That’s the part that bothers me. Me noticing something and speaking up about it isn’t the same as me creating the problem. I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. That’s what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been more than a year now.\n\nMaybe I haven’t always said things perfectly. Maybe I’ve sounded upset, because I am upset. But no matter how folks try to smooth it over and make it sound like family misunderstandings, I know what I’ve been seeing. If anything, my part is that I stopped just letting it happen quietly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could be presented to you that would completely change your beliefs about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what kind of evidence would completely change it, honestly. That’s hard, because this isn’t based on one little comment or one misunderstanding.\n\nI came here because, to me, my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that lets her get control of the money and pushes me out of decisions. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
If everyone said that to me… including my husband… I’d be hurt, first of all. I mean, very hurt. Because it would feel like everybody had decided it was easier to say I’m misreading things than to really look at what’s been happening.

Would it make me stop and think? Maybe. I’m not saying I wouldn’t feel shaken by that. If my husband sat there and said, “You’re misunderstanding her,” I’d probably go over every conversation in my head again, every time I was left out, every time something with the business or the money got handled and then I was told later like it was already settled. I’d ask myself, “Am I missing something?” because I’m not trying to be stubborn just for the sake of being stubborn.

But change what I believe? Not just because everyone said so, no. Because people can smooth things over in families. They can all agree on a version that keeps peace, especially when there’s money and a family business involved. And that’s exactly the problem to me — my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. I didn’t come to that from one bad day or one argument. I’ve been seeing it for more than a year now.

So if they all told me I was misinterpreting her intentions, I’d listen, but I’d still be thinking, “Then why does it keep ending up the same way?” Why am I the one left out? Why does she end up with more say? Why does everyone want me to just accept it and move along?

I know they might call that me not trusting them, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It would bother me deeply, yes, but it wouldn’t just erase the pattern.

After what I’ve seen, that would take a lot.\n\nBecause I came here for this exact reason — my husband’s sister has been working the family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. That’s the thing people keep acting like I don’t understand — like if somebody explains it nicer, or says, “Oh, she didn’t mean it that way,” then I’m supposed to just forget the pattern.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gets her more control over the money and leaves me out of decisions. And maybe I have pushed back more than I used to. I’ll admit that. I’ve asked questions, I’ve wanted to know where money was going, I’ve wanted to be included instead of hearing things after decisions were already made.\n\nBut I don’t think that means I caused it. That’s the part that bothers me. Me noticing something and speaking up about it isn’t the same as me creating the problem. I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. That’s what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been more than a year now.\n\nMaybe I haven’t always said things perfectly. Maybe I’ve sounded upset, because I am upset. If anything, my part is that I stopped just letting it happen quietly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could be presented to you that would completely change your beliefs about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what kind of evidence would completely change it, honestly. I can say I’d look at something if it was put in front of me—I’m not saying I would cover my eyes and refuse to hear anything—but completely change what I believe about her intentions? That’s hard, because this isn’t based on one little comment or one misunderstanding.\n\nI came here because, to me, my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that lets her get control of the money and pushes me out of decisions. And I’ve been seeing that for more than a year now. It’s been the way things get discussed without me, the way decisions seem already made, the way people try to smooth it over afterward like I’m just being sensitive or making trouble.\n\nSo if someone showed me papers or messages or whatever and said, “See, she didn’t mean anything by it,” I might listen, but I’d also be looking at the whole pattern. Because a person can make things look innocent on paper and still be steering things behind the scenes. Maybe if there was a long stretch where I was truly included, and the money wasn’t being handled around me, and she stopped ending up with more say every time—maybe that would make me question it more.\n\nBut completely change my belief right now? No, I don’t think there’s one piece of evidence that would do that. I know what I’ve been seeing, even if everybody wants to explain it away.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your husband's sister's actions would change if everyone in your family, including your husband, told you that you are misinterpreting her intentions?\n\nPatient: If everyone said that to me… including my husband… I’d be hurt, first of all. I mean, very hurt. Because it would feel like everybody had decided it was easier to say I’m misreading things than to really look at what’s been happening.\n\nWould it make me stop and think? Maybe. I’m not saying I wouldn’t feel shaken by that. If my husband sat there and said, “You’re misunderstanding her,” I’d probably go over every conversation in my head again, every time I was left out, every time something with the business or the money got handled and then I was told later like it was already settled. I’d ask myself, “Am I missing something?” because I’m not trying to be stubborn just for the sake of being stubborn.\n\nBut change what I believe? Not just because everyone said so, no. Because people can smooth things over in families. They can all agree on a version that keeps peace, especially when there’s money and a family business involved. And that’s exactly the problem to me — my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. I didn’t come to that from one bad day or one argument. I’ve been seeing it for more than a year now.\n\nSo if they all told me I was misinterpreting her intentions, I’d listen, but I’d still be thinking, “Then why does it keep ending up the same way?” Why am I the one left out? Why does she end up with more say? Why does everyone want me to just accept it and move along?\n\nI know they might call that me not trusting them, but I know what I’ve been seeing. I wouldn’t just believe it because she said she changed.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about your husband's sister's actions and their potential impact on you and the family business?\n\nPatient: A lot, honestly. I don’t sit there every single minute, but it’s in my head most days, because it affects everything, you know? It’s not just “oh, she said something rude” or some little family disagreement I can brush off. This is the family business, and the money, and who gets to make decisions, and whether I’m being pushed further and further out.\n\nSometimes I’ll be doing something ordinary and I’ll start thinking back over what happened, who said what, why she handled something a certain way, why I wasn’t told until later, that kind of thing. And then people want to smooth it over like it’s nothing, like I’m just upset, but I’ve been seeing the same pattern for more than a year now. So yes, I spend a good amount of time thinking about it, because if I don’t pay attention, she gets more control and I get left out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to ever completely trust your husband's sister again?\n\nPatient: Completely trust her again? I don’t know… that’s hard for me to say yes to. Maybe if things were different for a long time, maybe I could be civil and watch how she acts, but completely trust? After what I’ve seen, that would take a lot.\n\nBecause I came here for this exact reason — my husband’s sister has been working the family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. And people keep trying to smooth it over, like I’m just taking it personal or making more of it than it is, but I know what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on for more than a year now, not just one bad conversation or one mistake.\n\nSo no, I can’t just say I’d completely trust her again. Not unless she really stopped putting herself in the middle of everything and showed, over time, that she wasn’t trying to control the money or leave me out. Even then, I think part of me would still be watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people who might be working together with your husband's sister against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say for sure there are others working with her, like sitting in a room planning it all out together. I’m careful about saying that because then people act like I’m accusing everybody of something. But I do feel like people have helped smooth things over for her, or made excuses, or told me I’m reading too much into it when I’m the one who has been watching this happen.\n\nThe main person I’m worried about is my husband’s sister. That’s why I came here. She’s been using the family business to get more control over the money and push me out of decisions, and this has been going on for more than a year. It’s not one conversation or one misunderstanding. It’s a pattern.\n\nSo are there other people working with her? Maybe not directly, I don’t know. But when people keep covering it up or trying to make me let it go, it feels like they’re helping her, whether they mean to or not. And no matter how they try to smooth it over, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any amount of evidence or proof to the contrary could ever change your mind about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: I mean… if it was real proof, I guess I’d have to look at it, but I don’t know what kind of proof would change what I’ve already seen with my own eyes for this long. That’s the thing people keep acting like I don’t understand — like if somebody explains it nicer, or says, “Oh, she didn’t mean it that way,” then I’m supposed to just forget the pattern.\n\nI came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gets her more control over the money and leaves me out of decisions. And this hasn’t been one week or one little misunderstanding. It’s been more than a year of things being handled around me, decisions being made, and then everyone trying to smooth it over afterward like I’m just being sensitive.\n\nSo could evidence change my mind? Maybe if I saw, over a long time, that I was actually included and she wasn’t putting herself in the middle of the money and decisions anymore. But just people telling me I’m wrong? No. That wouldn’t change it. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your husband's sister's intentions?\n\nPatient: Could I be mistaken? I mean… I suppose anybody can be mistaken about some things. I’m not saying I’m perfect or that I never misunderstand a tone or a conversation. But this doesn’t feel like just me getting one thing wrong.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. And it’s not like it happened once and I got upset and held onto it. This has been going on for more than a year now, with things being handled around me and then people trying to smooth it over afterward, like I’m supposed to just accept it and not ask questions.\n\nSo could I be mistaken about every bit of it? No, I don’t really believe that. Maybe I could be wrong about what she says to herself in her own head, because I can’t read her mind. But I know what I’ve been seeing. I know the pattern. And when people keep trying to explain it away, that almost makes me feel more sure that something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about your husband's sister's actions and their impact on you and the family business every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, maybe not every single minute of the day, but it’s there every day because it affects everything. It’s not like some little family disagreement I can just put in a drawer and forget about.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way where she gets more control over the money and I get pushed out of decisions. And when that’s your family, and it’s the business, and it’s money, you can’t just not think about it. I’ll be doing something ordinary and then I’ll remember how something was handled without me, or how everybody tried to smooth it over afterward, and it starts turning in my mind again.\n\nThis has been going on for more than a year, so yes, I think about it a lot. People make it sound like I’m just obsessing or making trouble, but I know what I’ve been seeing. If someone kept stepping into your place and making decisions around you, you’d think about it too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your views about your husband's sister's actions could ever change, regardless of what she does?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “regardless of what she does,” because that makes it sound like I’m just refusing to see anything. I’m not trying to be unfair. If things actually changed — and I mean really changed, not just everybody telling me to calm down or saying I’m taking it wrong — then maybe over time I’d have to look at that.\n\nBut right now, no, my views aren’t just going to change because someone wants them to. I came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. That’s what it looks like, and it has looked like that for more than a year. It’s not one conversation or one bad day. It’s the way things keep getting handled, and then after the fact people try to smooth it over like it was nothing.\n\nSo if she truly stopped putting herself in the middle and I was actually included, maybe I’d feel differently after seeing that for a long while. But if you mean could I just wake up and decide I was wrong no matter what’s happened? No. I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to consider an alternative explanation for your husband's sister's actions if it were presented to you?\n\nPatient: I’d be willing to listen, yes, but I don’t want that to mean I’m agreeing ahead of time that I’ve imagined all of this. Because that’s what it feels like people want from me — they want to say, “Oh, maybe she didn’t mean it that way,” or “Maybe it’s just business,” or “Maybe you’re taking it too personally,” and then I’m supposed to just drop it.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. That’s not just a feeling I had one afternoon. It’s been more than a year of seeing things handled around me, and then when I bring it up, folks try to smooth it over like it’s nothing, like I’m the problem for noticing.\n\nSo, yes, you can present another explanation. I’m not saying I’d plug my ears. But it would have to explain the whole pattern, not just one little piece of it. Because no matter how everybody tries to soften it or make it sound innocent, I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything your husband's sister could do or say that would completely change your opinion of her intentions?\n\nPatient: Completely change it? I don’t know. That’s a hard one, because words are easy. She could say all kinds of things — “I never meant to,” “You misunderstood,” “I only wanted to help” — but I’ve heard versions of that already, or at least I’ve heard people around us trying to make it sound that way.\n\nWhat would change my opinion is not just something she says one time in a nice voice. I know what I’ve been seeing, no matter how folks try to smooth it over.\n\nDoctor: Would you consider the possibility that your husband's sister's actions, although harmful to you, are not entirely intentional?\n\nPatient: I can consider it in the sense that I can hear the words you’re saying, yes. Not with how long it’s gone on and how it keeps benefiting her.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have done anything to contribute to the situation with your husband's sister?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve asked myself that, because people keep making it sound like if I had just been quieter, or more agreeable, or not questioned things, then there wouldn’t be all this tension. And maybe I have pushed back more than I used to. I’ll admit that. I’ve asked questions, I’ve wanted to know where money was going, I’ve wanted to be included instead of hearing things after decisions were already made.\n\nBut I don’t think that means I caused it. I can say I’d look at something if it was put in front of me—I’m not saying I would cover my eyes and refuse to hear anything—but completely change what I believe about her intentions? I know what I’ve been seeing, even if everybody wants to explain it away.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your husband's sister's actions would change if everyone in your family, including your husband, told you that you are misinterpreting her intentions?\n\nPatient: If everyone said that to me… including my husband… I’d be hurt, first of all. I mean, very hurt. Because it would feel like everybody had decided it was easier to say I’m misreading things than to really look at what’s been happening.\n\nWould it make me stop and think? Maybe. I’m not saying I wouldn’t feel shaken by that. If my husband sat there and said, “You’re misunderstanding her,” I’d probably go over every conversation in my head again, every time I was left out, every time something with the business or the money got handled and then I was told later like it was already settled. Because people can smooth things over in families. They can all agree on a version that keeps peace, especially when there’s money and a family business involved. And that’s exactly the problem to me — my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that gives her more control over the money and pushes me out of decisions. I didn’t come to that from one bad day or one argument. I’ve been seeing it for more than a year now.\n\nSo if they all told me I was misinterpreting her intentions, I’d listen, but I’d still be thinking, “Then why does it keep ending up the same way?” Why am I the one left out? Why does she end up with more say? Why does everyone want me to just accept it and move along?\n\nI know they might call that me not trusting them, but I know what I’ve been seeing. It would bother me deeply, yes, but it wouldn’t just erase the pattern."
